#6700. Ayo Edebiri Got the Forehead Curl Memo

Ayo Edebiri is one of the best-dressed people in Hollywood. In 2024, GQ named her a rising star in menswear. At our 2026 Men of the Year party, she made an even stronger case for herself as a style icon for the ages. Walking the red carpet at the Chateau Marmont in West Hollywood, the Emmy-winning actor continued her winning streak of power suiting in a navy chalk-stripe ensemble, a bright emerald-turquoise dress shirt, and a burgundy geometric tie. Her bob was styled in a side part, with a pronounced curl sweeping across her forehead. It’s a look that’s become a staple among Hollywood’s best-dressed men this year, a callback to Superman’s signature swoop, minus the Spandex tights and cape.

In the 1920s, the playful wisp of hair was known as the kiss curl: a deliberately placed lock of hair that sat on the forehead, even amid an otherwise carefully sculpted ’do. (As Seth Rogen helpfully pointed out on Instagram after we noted the kiss curl’s 2026 resurgence: “It’s called a Forelock.”) Its prominence in men’s grooming can be traced back in part to the French-American entertainer Josephine Baker, whose iconic curl ushered in an era—and now a century—of rogue forehead curls.

The kiss curl was so emblematic of the era that it made a triumphant return at the 2025 Met Gala, themed “Superfine: Tailoring Black Style.” A host of women paired the look with impeccable tailoring, including Simone Biles, Taraji P. Henson, Teyana Taylor, and, yes, Ayo Edebiri.

The modern appeal of the forehead curl is easy to understand. As hairstyles have grown longer, looser, and less structured, the curl lends an air of studied nonchalance—as if you didn’t spend too much time getting your hair just right. It’s sexy in a just-got-out-of-bed kind of way, especially when everything else—from the rest of the hair to the outfit—remains crisp, clean, and pristine.

As Edebiri once told Vogue of dressing for the red carpet: “One of the fun parts of getting to do this sort of thing is that you get to transform and be these different permutations of yourself.” At GQ’s Men of the Year party, she once again understood the assignment.

#6697. Julia Fox Pairs Her Preppy Purple Look With Her Vintage Prada Bowling Bag

The Y2K accessory is one of her favorites

When it comes to her style, Julia Fox has never been afraid to make a statement. Whether turning belts into a bra and miniskirt, tucking her hair into a “Velveeta gold” wig, or putting a futuristic twist on naked dressing, the actor has pushed the boundaries of red-carpet dressing and street style alike. Just this month, she showed up to the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards in a tulle Miss Claire Sullivan dress that was made using world maps.

For her latest look, Fox turned her attention to designer Sofia Testino, piecing together a preppy purple fit full of the label’s pieces. On top, she wore the Air Hostess Polo, a fitted short-sleeve ivory top with a purple collar and stripe of green. It featured an elongated button placket and a rounded deep V neckline trimmed in purple and green.

The Down the Drain memoir author wore the polo with the coordinating Air Hostess Arrow Skirt—a purple leather midi pencil skirt with arrow motifs across the front—and a reversible jacket. The Work Woman Jacket, as it is called, was crafted with a structured silhouette and padded shoulders. One side was cut from a cream checked wool, while the inverse was made from pale-blue satin with contrast checked panels. The whole thing was finished with brown ribbed cuffs.

Fox added semi-sheer black tights and periwinkle patent-leather wedges, also from Sofia Testino. And for a bag, she brought her go-to geometric black-and-white bowling bag from the Prada Spring/Summer 2000 collection.

#6696. Derek Hough Reveals Why He Really Gave Everyone the Same Score on ‘DWTS’

Coach Jackie would definitely have kept an eye on that judges’ table.

It seems like Derek Hough’s rage-bait isn’t limited to Coach Jackie J. (IYKYK!) The Dancing with the Stars judge seems to have fans spiraling over his scores from last week’s Grammy Night performances.

Derek scored each couple a 9 for each of their performances throughout the night, and DWTS devotees were understandably curious as to why.

When pro dancer Ezra Sosa chatted with the judge during the after-show livestream on TikTok, Derek joked, “Listen, I’ve had three hours of sleep. I came from Seattle this morning on tour. I’m going to Florida right after this Disney special, and then back on tour…That’s why I think I’m giving nines to everybody today.”

The joke seemingly went over some people’s heads, as one person criticized the longtime judge for “flying under the radar” as the “nice guy.”

“Did derek actually say he was tired so he gave everyone a 9.. #dwts,” one person wrote, while another chimed in, “Yes!!!!! I can’t believe there isn’t more people talking about this right now!!!! That was so unprofessional not only that, but why are you acting like the semifinals aren’t on Tuesday??? Just handing 9s to whoever or whatever the dance was.”

Another person even joked that Coach Jackie should have been in the ballroom to “keep eyes on Derek.”

“What do you actually mean derek was ‘too tired to judge’ so he just gave everybody a nine,” they wrote alongside a clip of Jackie in the DWTS audience a few weeks prior.

What’s more: Derek retroactively shared a vlog recounting the day he got “three hours of sleep.”

“So this is why you were funky during the show 😭,” someone commented, while another criticized, “Next year, maybe delay the tour so that the contestants can be fairly judged by someone who isn’t just throwing in the towel and scoring all 9s because they’re tired.”

Derek has yet to comment further on the matter, if at all, but we’ll see what he brings to the judging panel during the semifinals on Tuesday!
